Let me summarize the current status:

1. **eval_grpo_g8_bf16** (cuda:3): ckpt-80 eval done (F1=0.4456), now running ckpt-90 eval — at 56% (35/62 batches). ETA ~25 min.

2. **plan_001_rsft_flat_nli** (cuda:0): 443/507 steps (87%), ~5s/step. ETA ~5 min. Loss ~0.62, epoch 2.37. Training looks stable.

3. **plan_012_dpo_ced** (cuda:2): 141/501 steps (28%), ~12s/step. ETA ~72 min. Loss values logged. Training seems steady.
   
4. **plan_014_sft_no_evidence** (cuda:1): 255/570 steps (45%), ~6.3s/step. ETA ~33 min. Just wrapped epoch 1 eval with eval_loss=0.8374. Loss hovering around 0.68-0.72, but I'm noticing entropy creeping up from 0.70 to 0.76 while mean_token_accuracy is drifting down slightly from 0.844 to 0.828.

5. **plan_015_grpo_g4_bf16_v2** (cuda:3): Just starting out at 6/200 steps (3%), running slow at ~135s/step. This one's going to take a while — looking at roughly 7.3 hours.

I'm seeing both the eval and plan_015 are sharing cuda:3 right now, pulling about 14.5GB and 14.3GB respectively for nearly 29GB total. The eval should wrap up in about 25 minutes, freeing up the GPU for plan_015 to run solo. Meanwhile, plan_001 is nearly done at 87% with just 5 minutes left, and the eval checkpoint is about halfway through at 56%.

The reward metrics for plan_015 look solid so far — format at 0.27, f1 at 0.99, ced at 0.38, totaling 1.64. The reward values in the log appear to be unweighted sums — adding them directly gives 0.27 + 0.99 + 0.38 = 1.64, which matches the total shown, so the individual components aren't being scaled by separate weights. which matches 1/1/1 weighting. The summary flags this as a potential confound—plan_015 supposedly uses 0.3/3.0/1.0 weights like plan_011, but the actual reward totals suggest the defaults (1.0/1.0/1.0) were applied instead. I need to verify whether the logged rewards are raw values before weighting or if the framework separates raw and weighted totals. I need to dig into the actual command logged for plan_015_v2 to see what weights were really used, since the registry entry is truncated. Looking at the log output, I can see it mentions CED reward with tau annealing, but the reward weights aren't explicitly stated there. Let me work backwards from the total reward value—if the logged reward of 1.64 represents the weighted sum, I can figure out which weights were actually applied.

_source line 912 · 2026-05-15T16:27:10.666Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Now I can see the `WeightedRewardFn` class:

```python
class WeightedRewardFn:
    """Wrap a reward function with a scalar weight."""

    def __init__(self, fn, weight: float):
        self.__name__ = getattr(fn, "__name__", fn.__class__.__name__)
        self.fn = fn
        self.weight = weight

    def __call__(self, *args, **kwargs):
        rewards = self.fn(*args, **kwargs)
        return [r * self.weight for r in rewards]
```

So the wrapper multiplies each reward by its weight before returning to TRL, meaning TRL receives already-weighted rewards from each function and logs them as-is. The total reward is just the sum of all these weighted per-function rewards. Looking at plan_015_v2, I can work backwards from the logged values: format_reward shows 0.27 (which is raw * 0.3), so the raw value was around 0.9, and f1_reward shows 0.99 (raw * 3.0), putting the raw value at approximately 0.33.

The weights are definitely being applied correctly—the logged total of 1.64 matches the sum of the weighted components. But this raises an interesting question about the original QLoRA GRPO baseline: if it used default weights of 1.0 for all three rewards, then those same raw values would've been logged as 0.9 and 0.33 instead of 0.27 and 0.99, meaning the reward signal composition is fundamentally different between the two approaches.

This creates a confound in the ablation—plan_015 was meant to isolate just the quantization difference while keeping everything else constant, but if the reward weights changed too, that's a second variable I need to account for. I should dig into the original QLoRA GRPO experiment configuration to see what weights it actually used.
````

Now I see the key data:

The first QLoRA GRPO experiment (`mvp2_grpo_ced`) at line 135 does NOT explicitly list reward weights in its command. The command is at line 148:
```
0.5 --output_dir /root/autodl-tmp/grpo_s3 --num_generations 4 --reward_device
```

This is truncated but it looks like it doesn't have `--format_reward_weight` etc. Let me read the full command for mvp2_grpo_ced.

Actually, looking at line 362: "→ added WeightedRewardFn wrapper + --format_reward_weight/--f1_reward_weight/--ced_reward_weight" — this note appears to be in mvp2_grpo_ced_r1's conclusion/notes, suggesting that the WeightedRewardFn was ADDED in r1 or after. 

So the original mvp2_grpo_ced likely relied on the default 1.0 weights for all reward components, while mvp2_grpo_ced_r1 introduced the WeightedRewardFn wrapper and switched to the 0.3/3.0/1.0 weight configuration.

Looking at the progression, the key breakthrough came with plan_011 when they switched to bf16 precision and increased the KL coefficient to 0.01—that's where the catastrophic forgetting finally stopped. The current experiment plan_015_v2 is testing whether reducing the learning rate to 5e-6 while keeping KL at zero can maintain that stability. I need to pull up the full registry entry for mvp2_grpo_ced to see exactly what hyperparameters it's using, since the kl coefficient discrepancy is crucial for determining whether plan_015 is truly isolating a single variable or if there are multiple confounds at play.

Now I have the key finding:

**mvp2_grpo_ced** (the original QLoRA GRPO, line 135-178) command does NOT include `--format_reward_weight`, `--f1_reward_weight`, or `--ced_reward_weight` flags. Since the defaults in grpo_trainer.py are all 1.0, this experiment used **1/1/1 weights**.

But this experiment actually failed (EXIT_CODE=1, AttributeError) at ~36min. It was a bug fix issue. So it never completed — it's not the one that showed catastrophic forgetting.


The catastrophic forgetting actually came from the later runs starting with mvp2_grpo_ced_r1, where the WeightedRewardFn wrapper and the explicit reward weight flags were introduced. Let me look at what r1 actually did.

Critical finding:

**mvp2_grpo_ced_r1** (the first QLoRA GRPO that actually ran long enough to show forgetting) also used **default 1/1/1 weights**. Its command (line 320-329) has NO `--format_reward_weight` etc. flags. It was during/after r1 that the WeightedRewardFn was added (as noted in line 362).

Mode collapse was detected at step 80/189 with F1=0.001. The fix in r1's notes explicitly says "Reward weighting: all 3 rewards (format/f1/ced) were equally summed by TRL → added WeightedRewardFn wrapper".
 
The progression shows the initial run failed due to a bug, then r1 exposed mode collapse with equal weighting before the WeightedRewardFn was introduced to address it. From r2 onward, the setup remained QLoRA with G=4 but experimented with different KL and learning rate configurations.

Now I'm comparing plan_015_v2 against mvp2_grpo_ced_kl001 to isolate the quantization effect—both use G=4 and the 0.3/3.0/1.0 weights, though they differ on KL penalty and learning rate, so I need to verify the exact hyperparameters to make sure the comparison is clean. ced_kl001 uses. Actually, the name suggests kl=0.01. Let me read its entry.

I need to figure out which specific QLoRA experiment plan_015 should be compared against. The context says it isolates quantization when compared to "original QLoRA GRPO", but I need to identify which one that is.

Looking at plan_015's hypothesis, it explicitly states it should match mvp2_grpo_ced exactly except for the quantization change. But checking mvp2_grpo_ced's actual parameters, there are mismatches: the learning rate is 5e-5 not 5e-6, and the loss weights are 1/1/1 not 0.3/3.0/1.0. So plan_015_v2 introduces two confounds compared to what it claims to be based on. I'm trying to identify which QLoRA GRPO experiment achieved that best rel_f1 score of 0.2167, and it looks like mvp2_grpo_ced_kl001 is the likely candidate. But now I'm realizing the plan_015 variants don't actually match the original mvp2_grpo_ced setup — they use different reward weights and learning rates, so the comparison isn't as clean as I thought.

The stable QLoRA runs (r2 through r5) all used the 0.3/3.0/1.0 weight configuration, so mvp2_grpo_ced_r3 would be a better baseline to compare against since it has the same weights and learning rate as plan_015_v1, just with a slightly higher KL penalty.

When I look at plan_015_v2, it matches on weights and G but diverges on KL (0.05 vs 0) and learning rate (5e-5 vs 5e-6), so there are still confounds even in this comparison. I should check mvp2_grpo_ced_kl001 since the name hints at KL=0.01, which would be closer to zero than 0.05 and might give me a cleaner comparison point.

Looking at the actual results, the weight change seems to have prevented mode collapse but not catastrophic forgetting, so it's a reasonable stabilization improvement. <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I see what happened:

1. **plan_015_grpo_g4_bf16_v2**: Status changed from RUNNING → PENDING (retry_count=1). It was "killed" (completed_at 16:25:20, previous_failure: killed). The worker likely killed v2 because of the cuda:3 conflict.

2. **plan_015_grpo_g4_bf16_v3**: New entry with status ALLOCATED, created at 16:27:09. Key difference: **kl_coef=0.01** (v2 had kl=0). 

The Agent corrected the kl coefficient from 0 to 0.01 in v3, but there's a problem: PID 223975 is still consuming 14.3GB on cuda:3 even though v2 was supposedly killed at 16:25:20, and v3 hasn't started running yet. This looks like another zombie process—the registry shows v2 as killed and v3 as allocated but not submitted, yet the GPU is still occupied.

More critically, changing kl_coef to 0.01 breaks the experimental design. The original plan was to keep kl=0 to isolate the quantization effect and match the baseline mvp2_grpo_ced setup, but now I'm seeing inconsistent kl values across the runs—the original had no kl_coef specified (defaulting to 0.0), r2 used 0.05, and now v3 is using 0.01. This introduces confounding variables instead of cleanly testing quantization. I see now—v2 and v3 are actually designed to test different variables. Version 2 isolates the quantization effect by matching the original QLoRA's settings (G=4, kl=0), while version 3 isolates the group size effect by keeping kl=0.01 consistent with plan_011 but reducing G from 8 to 4. So they're complementary ablations, not redundant ones.

The real issue is whether I should prioritize understanding the quantization impact or the group size impact, since removing v2 entirely means losing that quantization isolation experiment. I need to check if process 223975 is still actually running, and whether the cuda:3 conflict was the reason it was supposed to be terminated.

Looking at the eval progress, ckpt-90 was at 56% completion maybe 15-20 minutes ago, so it might be finished or nearly done by now. Let me check what's currently happening with both the eval and plan_015_v2.

_source line 989 · 2026-05-15T16:29:50.177Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
plan_001 is at 495/507 steps — about 1 minute from completion. Let me wait for it... actually, I should not wait. I should analyze what I've found and prepare my review.

Key findings from this analysis:

**1. The cleanest quantization ablation is plan_011 vs mvp2_grpo_ced_kl001_r4:**

| Parameter | plan_011 (bf16) | mvp2_grpo_ced_kl001_r4 (QLoRA) |
|---|---|---|
| Quantization | bf16 LoRA | QLoRA 4-bit NF4 |


| G | 8 | 8 |
| kl_coef | 0.01 | 0.01 |
| lr | 5e-5 | 5e-5 |
| weights | 0.3/3.0/1.0 | 0.3/3.0/1.0 |
| num_iterations | 1 | 1 |
| max_steps | 100 | 100 |
| grad_acc | 16 | 16 |
| Result | rel_f1=0.4549 | forgetting (rel_f1=0.2167) |

The quantization approach clearly hurts performance — the QLoRA variant shows significant catastrophic forgetting compared to the bf16 baseline, which is a major red flag for this technique.

Now looking at plan_015_v2 versus mvp2_grpo_ced, the comparison isn't actually clean since they differ in multiple ways beyond just quantization. The learning rate is an order of magnitude lower in plan_015_v2, and the loss weights diverge as well, so I can't isolate quantization as the sole variable here.

What's more, mvp2_grpo_ced had a bug and its fixed version mvp2_grpo_ced_r1 collapsed during training, so neither baseline completed normally. For a proper ablation, I should use plan_011 versus mvp2_grpo_ced_kl001_r4 as the main comparison since that's the only clean single-variable test, with plan_015 serving as supporting evidence.

The hypothesis framing for plan_015 needs correction though—it shouldn't be presented as a controlled experiment when it has multiple confounds. This is worth flagging before the paper goes out, even though the clean ablation already exists.

Now I need to find the actual eval results for mvp2_grpo_ced_kl001_r4 to see what the relative F1 score was, since the training metrics only show the reward signal.
```

Key findings:

1. **plan_015_v2 is a ZOMBIE again**: Registry says PENDING/killed, but PID 223975 is alive and still training (step 8/200, ~153s/step). The worker "killed" it in registry but didn't actually kill the process. This is the THIRD time plan_015 has had a zombie problem.

2. **eval_grpo_g8_bf16 ckpt-90**: At 61% (38/62 batches). Still running. Notably the inference speed has DEGRADED significantly — last batches at ~97-102s/it, compared to 50-55s/it earlier. 

The slowdown is almost certainly because plan_015_v2 is hogging cuda:3, pushing memory usage to 88% of the 32.6GB available.

3. **plan_015_v3 introduced a kl coefficient of 0.01**: The agent modified the KL penalty from zero in v2 to 0.01 in v3, which fundamentally changes what we're testing — v2 was meant to isolate the quantization effect, but v3 now conflates that with KL regularization differences.

4. **The conflict resolution didn't fully execute**: The worker marked v2 as PENDING but never actually terminated the process, so it's still consuming resources. Meanwhile v3 sits in ALLOCATED status waiting to be submitted.

The critical issues are that v2's process is still running and wasting GPU memory, and v3's parameter changes have muddied the experimental comparison we were trying to make.

There's a tradeoff here—v2 represents the original quantization isolation ablation we wanted, but it's also slowing down the eval checkpoint. The registry claims it's killed, but it's clearly not, which makes the whole state inconsistent.

Let me check back on the reward weight configurations to see if there's another issue I was tracking with the different experiment runs. So the weight mismatch only matters if we're comparing v2 against those earlier runs with different settings—if the forgetting showed up in r2+ with matching weights, then v2's configuration is consistent with that. Regardless, I need to address the immediate problems: the zombie process still running on cuda:3, the experimental design shift in v3, and the evaluation bottleneck, then report these to Director.

- Config: bf16, G=8, kl=0.01, lr (same hyperparameters as QLoRA except for precision)

Since both experiments use identical hyperparameters except for quantization method, this is a clean ablation study. The results are striking: QLoRA 4-bit causes severe catastrophic forgetting with a relative F1 drop of 18.9 percentage points below the SFT baseline, while bf16 actually improves performance by 4.9 percentage points. This makes it clear that quantization is the critical factor driving the performance difference.

Let me verify there aren't other confounding variables. Both runs used a single GPU (cuda:0 for the QLoRA run, cuda:3 for bf16), so hardware allocation isn't a factor. The reward device settings are also consistent. Each evaluation correctly used its corresponding precision—QLoRA eval with the quantize flag and bf16 eval without it—so the inference setup matches the training precision appropriately.

Now I'm pulling together the key findings from this comparison. The data shows a clean ablation: plan_011 and mvp2_grpo_ced_kl001_r4 are nearly identical except for quantization, with QLoRA producing rel_f1=0.2167 and bf16 producing rel_f1=0.4549. This strongly suggests quantization is driving the catastrophic forgetting. But I also need to flag that plan_015_v2's claim about being identical to the original QLoRA GRPO run doesn't hold up—there are actual differences I need to document. Let me check if plan_015_v2 is actually the only candidate for a clean G=4 ablation with the modified weights, or if there's another QLoRA experiment I'm missing that could serve the same purpose. Scanning through the experiment configs, I need to verify the exact hyperparameters for the recent runs to see if any of them already isolate the variables I care about. and plan_015 serves as a supplementary check even though it has some confounding variables. I've got the data I need to write up Review 7.

Now I have the full picture:

**mvp2_grpo_ced_kl001**: QLoRA 4-bit, G=4, kl=0.01, lr=5e-5, weights 0.3/3.0/1.0 → rel_f1=0.2167 (catastrophic forgetting)

So the experiment that produced the "best" QLoRA GRPO result (rel_f1=0.2167) used:
- Quantization: QLoRA 4-bit


- G: 4
- kl_coef: 0.01
- lr: 5e-5
- Weights: 0.3/3.0/1.0

But when I look at plan_015_v2 (currently running as a zombie process), it's supposed to isolate the quantization effect with bf16, G=4, kl=0, lr=5e-6, weights 0.3/3.0/1.0 — except that's actually three confounds at once: the quantization changed, the KL coefficient went from 0.01 to 0, and the learning rate dropped from 5e-5 to 5e-6. This isn't a clean isolation at all.

plan_015_v3 is allocated but not running yet with bf16, G=4, kl=0.01, lr=5e-6, weights 0.3/3.0/1.0 — that's better, only two confounds (quantization plus the learning rate change). But wait, I'm seeing a discrepancy in the context summary. It claims plan_015_v2 has lr=5e-6 to match the original QLoRA GRPO mvp2_grpo_ced exactly, just changing quantization from 4-bit to bf16. But mvp2_grpo_ced actually used lr=5e-5, not 5e-6, so something's off with that claim.

Let me trace back more carefully. The original mvp2_grpo_ced_r1 (the first one that actually ran) had QLoRA 4-bit, G=4, kl=0, lr=5e-5, and default weights of 1/1/1. Then mvp2_grpo_ced_kl001 introduced kl=0.01 and changed the weights to 0.3/3.0/1.0 while keeping everything else the same, and that's what scored 0.2167. Now plan_015_v2 is switching to bf16 and dropping the learning rate to 5e-6, which introduces multiple changes at once.

Looking at the comparisons, v2 has three confounds against the original (quantization type, learning rate, and weights), and three confounds against ced_kl001 (quantization, kl value, and learning rate). The v3 variant with kl=0.01 restored would be cleaner against ced_kl001 with just two confounds, but the learning rate mismatch still persists in both versions.

The real issue is that r0/r1 never completed successfully, so ced_kl001 is actually the first meaningful baseline to compare against—not the original buggy runs. That means I need to accept that ced_kl001 is my reference point, and the confounds I'm dealing with are the learning rate shift and the weight adjustments. I'm realizing there's a fundamental inconsistency here — the hypothesis claims plan_015_v2 matches the original mvp2_grpo_ced exactly except for quantization, but the learning rates are completely different (5e-6 vs 5e-5). The v1 variant with the correct learning rate was killed due to resource contention, leaving only the "safe" v2 with the wrong lr. Looking back at the registry, neither the original QLoRA experiment nor the kl=0.01 variant actually used 5e-6, so I'm not sure where that learning rate even came from.

Rather than chase this further, the critical issues are clear: there's a zombie process still running on cuda:3 that should be dead, and the experimental design is compromised because v3 changes the kl parameter, which breaks the isolation needed to properly test quantization effects against the baseline. I've got a decision to make about whether to let the zombie v2 process finish running or kill it and start v3 instead. Even with the learning rate mismatch, v2 would still give me some useful data, but I need to weigh that against the eval slowdown it's causing. Let me post this review and figure out the next steps.

[severity: warning] plan_015_v2 hypothesis 描述不准确，存在多变量 confound**\n\nplan_015_v2 hypothesis 声称：\"与原始 QLoRA GRPO mvp2_grpo_ced **完全一致，仅改 quantization**: 4-bit → bf16\"\n\n实际核实 mvp2_grpo_ced 原始命令（registry line 140-150），发现 plan_015_v2 与之有 **3 个变量差异**：\n\n| 参数 | plan_015_v2 | mvp2_grpo_ced (原始) |\n|------|------------|---------------------|\n| quantization | bf16 | QLoRA 4-bit |\n| reward weights | **0.3/3.0/1.0** | **1/1/1 (默认)** |\n| lr | **5e-6** | **5e-5** |\n\n证据链：\n- mvp2_grpo_ced 命令中无 `--format_reward_weight`/`--f1_reward_weight`/`--ced_reward_weight` 参数 → 使用代码默认值 1.0/1.0/1.0\n- `WeightedRewardFn` 是在 mvp2_grpo_ced_r1 之后才加入的（registry line 362 注释：\"added WeightedRewardFn wrapper\"）\n- plan_015_v2 实际命令（/proc/223975/cmdline 确认）使用 `--format_reward_weight 0.3 --f1_reward_weight 3.0 --ced_reward_weight 1.0 --learning_rate 5e-6`\n- mvp2_grpo_ced 使用 `--learning_rate 5e-5`\n\n更关键的是：mvp2_grpo_ced 本身 **因 bug 失败**（f1_reward_fn NoneType error, EXIT_CODE=1），从未正常完成训练。mvp2_grpo_ced_r1（同配置 bug 修复后）在 step 80 mode collapse 被 kill，也无完整 eval 数据。**plan_015_v2 没有可用的对照组。**\n\n---\n\n**3. [severity: info] plan_015_v2 仍有科学价值，但定位应为 \"robustness check\" 而非 \"controlled ablation\"**\n\n如果 plan_015_v2 (bf16, G=4, kl=0, lr=5e-6) 最终 rel_f1 不崩塌，它证明 bf16 的抗遗忘效果在 G=4/kl=0 条件下也成立（不仅限于 plan_011 的 G=8/kl=0.01 配置）。这是 robustness evidence，论文中应定位为 \"bf16 across different hyperparameter regimes\" 而非 \"controlled single-variable experiment\"。\n\n### 建议\n\n1. **论文 quantization ablation 应主打 plan_011 vs mvp2_grpo_ced_kl001_r4**（已有干净单变量对照，无需等 plan_015）\n2. **修正 plan_015_v2 的 registry hypothesis 描述**——删除\"完全一致，仅改 quantization\"的表述，改为如实描述与 plan_011 配置的差异\n3. plan_015_v2 结果可作为 supplementary evidence，但不应在论文中声称为 \"isolation experiment\"\n\n### GPU 状态\n\n4 卡全部活跃，无越界。plan_001 训练 495/507 (98%)，约 1 分钟内完成。eval_grpo_g8_bf16 ckpt-90 推理 56%。plan_015_v2 step 6/200 (3%)。",
